1. What the agent does

The SEO Agent is an enterprise SEO automation built as n8n workflows. One public form (or one API call) gives a customer:

  • I know my keyword — competitor research, a GO / GO_WITH_CHANGES / AVOID verdict, a keyword report and a finished, QA-checked page (Word + PDF).
  • Suggest keywords for my business — multi-source keyword research with an opportunity score, AI relevance screen, topic clusters, AI search demand and a live SERP check; form users then pick the keyword to write for.
  • Just describe my website — a structured description of the site from its homepage.
  • Audit my website — a technical Site Audit (crawl, probes, scoring) or a Full SEO Report (plus competitors, keywords, backlinks, PageSpeed, E-E-A-T content review, AI visibility).
  • Check if my keyword is right — the verdict alone.
  • Rank my site for a keyword (v4.2) — the keyword ladder: feasibility of the destination keyword, a ladder of 8-12 pages from winnable long-tail terms up to the head term with an internal link map and timeline, the first page written now, weekly position tracking with the recommended next rung.
  • Track my site (rankings & traffic) (v4.3) — weekly Site Tracker report from Google Search Console, GA4, Google Trends and live Google checks, with actions; optional blog posts per week (Content Cadence) and monitor settings.
  • I published a page (v4.3) — live check of a published page (meta, schema, author, images, links, indexability), the page marked published in the ladder and the content log, link suggestions.
  • Search Console check-in (monthly) (v4.3) — records manual actions, security issues and the Pages report, which the API cannot see.
  • Set up my business profile and Write a case study (v4.4) — author, reviewer and address for E-E-A-T and LocalBusiness schema on every page; a stored case study becomes proof for later pages and its own page.
  • Check my AI visibility and Check my backlinks (v4.5) — on-demand runs of the AI Visibility Tracker and the Backlink Monitor.

In the background, per tracked site: AI Visibility Tracker (Monday 07:00), Backlink Monitor (07:30), Rank Tracker (08:00), Site Tracker (09:00), Content Cadence (10:00), Audit Scheduler (1st of the month 06:00), and the Console Alerts mail watcher. Every audit carries the brand & entity check, the diff since the last audit, internal links to add and a fix pack (robots.txt, llms.txt, redirect map, schema, internal links). Since v4.6 stored results are reused where they cannot have changed (seo_cache, trends, rank history, AI answers, backlink history), with a safety net where a change could be invisible.

Data comes from DataForSEO (SERP, Labs, On-Page, Backlinks, WHOIS, Google Trends, Business Data, AI Optimization: LLM Responses, ChatGPT scraper, AI Mode, LLM Mentions), Google Search Console and GA4 (service account), Jina Reader (pages → markdown), Google PageSpeed Insights and RDAP; reasoning from Claude (Opus 5.5 for brief, copy and editing; Sonnet 5.5 for analysis, verdicts, relevance and critique; Haiku 4.5 for the site description). Reports are rendered to PDF by Gotenberg and delivered by e-mail (Gmail SMTP), form download or API callback.

2. Architecture and workflows

14 workflows live in the n8n project (658 nodes without notes). The main workflow holds every mode; the scheduled workflows (trackers, cadence, monitors, audit scheduler) work per site from the Data Tables; the rest are small helpers. Sub-runs (ladder and cadence pages, discovery follow-ups, case-study pages, form and scheduled audits) are started through API Entry as separate executions, which keeps form flows short and lets every stage deliver on its own. The state column is read from the running n8n (capture_runs.py).

Schedules

WhenWorkflowWhat it does per site
Monday 07:00AI Visibility TrackerBuyer questions × ChatGPT, Perplexity, Google AI Mode and AI Overviews; Gemini, Claude and the brand question on the month's first run (carried in between); market view monthly
Monday 07:30Backlink MonitorLight watch (lost / new / spammy links, alert only when it matters); full report on the month's first run; link gap quarterly
Monday 08:00Rank TrackerLadder keywords: live pages, the head term and anything already ranking weekly; unpublished pages not in the top 50 monthly
Monday 09:00Site TrackerSearch Console, GA4, trends (reused for 25 days), live checks (monthly for keywords Search Console reports), index status, AI / backlink / audit block, actions
Monday 10:00Content CadenceBlog posts per week: next ladder rung → striking-distance query → rising trend; each page as its own content run
1st of the month 06:00Audit SchedulerSitemap fingerprint; technical audit only when the site changed, 60+ days passed, never audited or no dated sitemap
Every few minutesConsole AlertsIMAP watcher: Search Console notification mails → typed alerts and an owner e-mail

How a request flows

  1. Entry: form page (basic auth) or POST /webhook/seo-keyword-check (header auth) → the API workflow validates, answers 202 and starts the main workflow through API Entry.
  2. Intake: Normalize Input → Rate Limit (per-address limit, estimated Claude budget) → Input OK? (errors become a form page or a callback rejection).
  3. Site read when needed (Jina + Claude Haiku) → Route Mode.
  4. Mode pipeline (discovery · keyword/verdict/ladder · audit · describe) → report builder → Gotenberg PDF.
  5. Delivery: e-mail (SMTP), form completion page (download) or callback JSON with PDF/Word bytes; the run ledger (DataForSEO spend per node, AI calls, duration) rides along.
  6. Follow-ups as separate executions: ladder pages, discovery report/content/audit, form audits (a form completion page ends its execution, so nothing may be queued behind it).
Design rules learned the hard way (all enforced in the build): every model node and every DataForSEO/Jina/PageSpeed HTTP node carries a credential slot; Code nodes read binaries with getBinaryDataBuffer; thinking tokens count against max tokens (brief 24k, copy/edit 32k, streaming on above ~21k); Form completion nodes are always last; error workflows and sub-workflows must be published; script-created Webhook nodes need a webhookId; text HTTP responses arrive under data, not body; crawls report www. URLs while canonicals may not (compare hosts loosely); the Code sandbox has no URL constructor; an AI Agent node outputs only {output} (read context with $('Node')); Data Table create nodes output the table, and upserts must receive exactly the table's columns; Send Email 2.1 attaches files only through fileAttachments, and every e-mail retries 3×; $env is blocked in nodes (settings are baked in from n8n/.env at build time).

3. Repository, build, test and deploy

PathPurpose
build_v4.pyBuilds the main workflow from the v3 export with asserted, anchored patches (sections 1-20: v4.1 fixes, v5 content, ladder, tracking, cadence, Search Console, check-in, E-E-A-T, monitors, no repeated work); writes workflows/SEO_Agent_v4.json, harness/code_v4/, CHANGES_v3_to_v4.md; then runs build_api.py, build_ladder_extras.py, build_site_tracker.py and build_monitors.py.
build_api.py · build_ladder_extras.py · build_site_tracker.py · build_monitors.py · ladder_common.pyAPI front door; Rank Tracker, WordPress publisher, Test Runner; Site Tracker, Site Admin, Content Cadence, Console Alerts; AI Visibility Tracker, Backlink Monitor, Audit Scheduler (inlining v5/code/_monitor_sites.js and _report_kit.js); shared Data Table columns.
apply_env.py · sync_credentials.py · env_settings.pySettings and secrets from n8n/.env: rebuild with brand / sender / ops address / service-account e-mail, write the credential records (fixed ids), import, publish, restart.
build_docs.py · capture_runs.pyThis guide (HTML; --pdf renders it through Gotenberg inside the n8n container) and the export of live run traces and workflow states into docs/runs/.
v5/prompts/*.txt · v5/code/*.jsAgent prompts and new Code nodes (read by the build). Edit here, never in the JSON.
harness/Offline test harness: runs every Code node in the n8n container's Node against fixtures (no paid calls). Must end "0 failed".
workflows/*.jsonGenerated workflow JSON (import with the ids inside). SEO_Agent_v3_original_export.json is the untouched source.
HANDOVER.md · REVIEW.md · *_FEATURE_SPEC.md · docs/Current state and how to use every mode; findings, every change, live test log, roadmap; the ladder, tracking and monitors specs with decisions; this guide and the exported run traces.

Build

cd n8n/seo-agent && python3 build_v4.py        # check the exit code: patches assert the exact text they replace

Test (offline)

cd harness
docker cp . n8n-n8n-1:/tmp/harness && docker exec -u root n8n-n8n-1 chown -R node:node /tmp/harness
docker exec n8n-n8n-1 sh -c 'cd /tmp/harness && CODE_DIR=/tmp/harness/code_v4 OUT_DIR=/tmp/harness/out node scenarios_v5.js'   # expect "514 passed, 0 failed"

Deploy

cd n8n && python3 seo-agent/apply_env.py      # the normal path: rebuild with .env settings, write credentials, import, publish, restart
# by hand:
docker exec n8n-n8n-1 n8n import:workflow --input=/tmp/SEO_Agent_v4.json --projectId=wo1WpMiHjdduGvfl   # upsert by id
# activation: POST /api/v1/workflows/<id>/activate with an n8n API key, or without a key:
docker exec n8n-n8n-1 n8n publish:workflow --id=SEOagentV4Full01 && docker restart n8n-n8n-1            # CLI publish takes effect after a restart
# verify without logging in: GET /form/<Start Form webhookId> → 401 (registered, auth required); POST /webhook/seo-keyword-check → 403

Credentials are created/updated without the UI through n8n import:credentials --input=<json> --projectId=… (upsert by id, encrypted on import). Never export or print credentials.

4. Configuration, guards and credentials

CONFIG (top of Normalize Input)

const CONFIG = {
  brand: "Dev SEO",   // from n8n/.env (SEO_BRAND) at build time; shown on reports and e-mails
  crawl_max_pages: 200,             // DataForSEO on-page crawl size (also used when reading crawled pages)
  crawl_js: false,                  // true = render JavaScript during the crawl (slower, costs more)
  qa_max_rounds: 2,   // one editor pass: live tests showed a second pass inflates length without raising the score                 // how many times the Editor may revise content that fails QA
  default_country: 'United States',
  ai_budget_usd: 10,                // estimated Claude spend allowed per budget period (see Rate Limit); raised from 3 on 2026-10-01 at the user's request to finish the system test — the Anthropic Console workspace limit is the hard cap
  ai_budget_period: 'day',          // 'day' | 'month' — daily window while testing; the Anthropic Console workspace limit is the hard cap
  ladder_pages_default: 1,          // ladder mode: pages written immediately (1-3; each page is its own content run)
  ladder_tracker_cadence: 'weekly', // the Rank Tracker workflow runs weekly; 'monthly' is documentation only until its schedule is changed
  ladder_auto_next_rung: false,     // false = the tracker recommends the next rung (one-click form link + API body); true is not wired
  wordpress_publish: false          // true + wordpress_url in the request = every generated page is also created as a WordPress DRAFT
};

Rate Limit

const LIMITS = { per_key_per_day: 6, global_per_day: 150 };
const EST = { content: 1.2, discover: 0.35, verdict: 0.2, full: 0.25, site_audit: 0, describe: 0.03, ladder: 0.55, report: 0.3, track: 0, published: 0, checkin: 0, profile: 0, case_study: 0, ai_visibility: 0.06, backlinks: 0.04 };

Keys: e-mail, else client IP (API) or the ladder/discover tag, else domain or keyword. The AI budget is an estimate-based guard (streaming model calls report no token usage); the Anthropic Console workspace limit is the real cap. The cap was raised 3 → 10 at the user's request on 2026-10-01 to finish the system test.

API

POST /webhook/seo-keyword-check, header X-API-Key. Body: mode (keyword | verdict | audit | discover | describe | ladder | track | published | checkin | profile | case_study | ai_visibility | backlinks), keyword, country (name or ISO-2), domain, business, customers, business_facts, cta, tone, goal, page_type, competitors, receive ["Keyword Report", "Page Content"], report_type, pages_now (ladder, 1-3), crawl_pages / crawl_js (audit), keywords / ga4_property_id / pages_per_week / competitors / monitors (track), published_url (published), profile and case-study fields, video_url / transcript / local_area (content), topics (ai_visibility), wordpress_url, email, callback_url (https; http only for localhost while testing). Answers 202 {status: accepted, request_id, estimated_minutes} or 400.

Callbacks (POST to callback_url, same request_id): stage = site_description · keyword_strategy · content (verdict, keyword data, QA summary, ladder_id/rung/head when part of a ladder, run_ledger, pdf + file as base64) · site_audit · full_report · ladder_plan (head, feasibility, rungs, top_page, link_map, timeline, write_now, requirements, stats, pdf/file) · rank_tracker (progress; positions, rungs, gains, drops, next_step with a ready-to-send api_body) · site_tracker_setup / site_tracker · content_cadence · published · console_checkin / console_alert · profile · case_study_started · ai_visibility_started / ai_visibility · backlinks_started / backlinks. Rejections: {status: rejected, request_id, error}.

Ladder page runs carry force_content, ladder_id, ladder_rung, ladder_head, ladder_page_no, ladder_links (top page + sibling; planned pages are allowed link targets).

6. Main workflow, stage by stage

Every node of SEO Agent v4 grouped by stage, in pipeline order. Descriptions come from the node itself (leading comment of the Code, HTTP target, IF condition, agent prompt, form fields) so they stay true to the build.

Entry points & forms

A 13-option public form (basic auth) or the Execute Workflow Trigger "API Entry", which the API front door, the ladder and cadence page runs, the discovery follow-ups, the case-study runs and the audit spawns all use. Each form option opens its own page of fields (the pages of the v4.3-v4.5 options are listed with their stages below).

NodeTypeWhat it does
Start FormForm triggerForm page with fields: What do you want?
credential: SEO Form Login
Choose PathSwitchSwitch: outputs keyword, discover, describe, audit, verdict_check, ladder, track, published, checkin, case_study, profile, ai_visibility, backlinks + fallback
Page KeywordForm pageForm page with fields: Target Keyword, Target Country, Page Type, Website Domain, Existing page URL (optional), City or area (local pages only), Video URL for this page (optional), Video transcript (optional), Business facts to include (optional), Main call to action (optional), Tone of voice, Content goal, Extra Features, What do you want to receive?, Email me the report
Page DiscoverForm pageForm page with fields: What does your business do?, Who are your customers?, Website Domain, Content goal, Business facts to include (optional), Target Country, Extra Features, What do you want to receive?, Email me the reports
Page DescribeForm pageForm page with fields: Website Domain, Email me the report
Page AuditForm pageForm page with fields: Website Domain, Target Country, Report type, Pages to crawl, Render JavaScript, Main competitors, Email me the report
Page VerdictForm pageForm page with fields: Target Keyword, Target Country, What does your business do?, Website Domain, Email me the result

Intake, validation & guards

Normalize Input turns any form page or API body into one normalised object (mode, keyword, domain, country codes, deliverables, ladder fields, CONFIG values). Validation problems are returned as data, not thrown; Rate Limit enforces the per-address daily limit and the estimated Claude budget; Input OK? routes errors to a form page or to the caller's callback.

NodeTypeWhat it does
Normalize InputCodev4: validation errors no longer crash the run. They are returned as { validation_error } and shown to the user (form completion page) or returned as HTTP 400 (API). See "Input OK?" right after Rate Limit.
Rate LimitCodeSimple abuse guard: limits runs per requester and per day (state lives in workflow static data; it persists only for active/production executions, not manual test runs).
Input OK?IFRoutes on: {{ !$json.validation_error }}
Error Via API?IFRoutes on: {{ !!$json.via_webhook }}
API RejectCodeAn API request failed validation inside the main run. Tell the caller through callback_url; with no callback_url, fail loudly so the error workflow alerts ops.
POST RejectionHTTP RequestHTTP POST {{ $json.callback_url }}
Show Input ErrorForm pageForm completion page: "Something needs fixing". Ends the form flow (the execution stays in waiting).

Site read & description

When the business is not described, the homepage is read through Jina Reader and summarised by Claude Haiku into a structured site description (services, audience, USPs, seed keywords); unreadable sites get an honest fallback. v4.6: a description stored in the last 30 days (seo_cache desc:<domain>) is reused instead of reading the site again; every fresh description is saved. "Just describe my website" always reads fresh, and what the user typed always wins.

"Just describe my website" always reads the site fresh, and what the user typed always wins.

Keyword discovery

Seeds by buyer motive → seven DataForSEO Labs pulls (ideas, long-tail, related, live SERP, competitor rankings) → one pool with an opportunity score → Claude relevance screen → clusters, quick wins, questions → AI search demand → live SERP check of the priority keywords → Keyword Strategy report (Word + PDF). Form users then pick the keyword on "Choose your keyword"; the follow-ups they ticked (report / content / audit) start as separate executions.

Keyword pipeline: research

Used by keyword, verdict and ladder runs. Sitemaps give real internal-link targets and detect an existing page; the client's own pages are read for facts; the live SERP (top 30, PAA, AI Overview) picks competitors whose pages are read (with a browser-engine retry for bot-protected pages) and distilled into a term model, questions and statistics; a second search collects verified facts with sources; Claude analyses the competition; keyword metrics and site authority complete the picture.

NodeTypeWhat it does
Prepare Keyword RunCodeSingle entry point for the keyword pipeline (SERP -> competitors -> brief -> content). Reached from Route Mode (keyword mode) or from the Discover fork (top suggested keyword).

Parse Sitemap IndexCodeInput: the fetched /sitemap.xml (text). Output: the sitemap files to read (max 5).
Fetch SitemapsHTTP RequestHTTP GET {{ $json.url }}
Collect Site URLsCodeBuilds the list of REAL pages on the user's site (from sitemap files) so the brief and the copywriter only link to URLs that exist, and detects whether a page for this keyword already exists.
Read Client Pages?IFRoutes on: {{ !!$json.domain }}
Client Page RequestsCodeRead the client's own pages (existing page on the topic, homepage, one related service page) so the brief and the copy are grounded in real facts instead of placeholders. One item per URL for "Read Client Pages".

Verdict

Claude Sonnet weighs demand, difficulty, link strength, intent, SERP features and the site's authority into GO / GO_WITH_CHANGES / AVOID with reasons, what must change, expected visits and time to rank. Ladder runs branch here; AVOID stops content unless the run is a forced ladder page.

Content context: profile, proof, page role, video (v4.4)

Before the brief, every content run loads the site's business profile (author, expert reviewer, address / NAP) and its recorded case studies from the Data Tables seo_profiles / seo_case_studies, reads the video page when the request carries a video (YouTube watch page or Vimeo oEmbed: title, upload date, duration), and Brief Context derives the page role (hub for the ladder's top page or a pillar page, case study, local page, standard), the cluster pages a hub links down to, and the prompt blocks for the brief and the copy (author and experience, proof, role rules, video).

On-demand monitors and audit upgrades (v4.5)

Check my AI visibility / Check my backlinks (form + API) start the monitor workflows at once for one domain; Track my site stores the monitor settings (seo_monitors). Every audit: brand & entity check against the Google Business Profile (a listing counts only with the site's website or phone) and the homepage Organization schema; audit history with the since-the-last-audit diff; internal links to add; the fix pack (robots.txt, llms.txt, redirect map, schema, internal links; zipped for the e-mail).

Business profile & case studies (v4.4)

Two free-standing modes. "Set up my business profile" / API mode profile merges the submitted fields over the stored row (empty keeps, "-" clears), upserts seo_profiles and reports the E-E-A-T and local readiness with a Person / LocalBusiness preview; free and never rate-limited. "Write a case study" / API mode case_study stores the intake in seo_case_studies (proof for later pages), logs the page in seo_content_log and starts the case-study page as its own content run (page type Case Study, force_content, internal key casestudy:); "I published a page" later marks the case study published so other pages may link it.

Content generation & QA

Opus writes the brief and the page from the research; Sonnet critiques; the deterministic QA computes a content score (structure, topic coverage, readability, specificity, clean language, length), fixes what it can, generates the schema (Article, Person, Service, LocalBusiness, VideoObject, ItemList, BreadcrumbList, FAQPage by page role) and asks the Opus editor for one revision when needed. v4.6: the editor pass is skipped only when the draft already scores 90+, the review rates it 85+ with no high-severity problem, the length is on target and only style notes remain; every SEO check still forces it.

Keyword ladder

Four DataForSEO pulls around the head term → topic-filtered pool with opportunity score → Claude relevance anchored on the head term → rungs by difficulty (1: ≤25 long-tail, 2: 26-45, 3: 46-60, top = head), one page per topic cluster, existing pages matched, internal link map, timeline, feasibility → Keyword Ladder Plan (Word + PDF) → rows into the Data Table seo_ladders → delivery → the first page(s) start as separate content runs.

8. Offline test harness

harness/scenarios_v5.js runs every Code node of the workflows against realistic fixtures (DataForSEO, Jina, PageSpeed, LLM outputs) in the n8n container's Node runtime, with a mini n8n executor ($input, $('Node'), $runIndex, static data, getBinaryDataBuffer). Scenarios: validation and rate limit; keyword mode (full content pipeline, truncated output, AVOID path, empty SERP); verdict; describe; discovery (research pool, relevance, clusters, strategy report, choice step, spawned follow-ups); full report and technical audit (crawl, probes incl. apex→www, HTML/schema, competitors, keywords, backlinks, PageSpeed, content review, AI visibility, scoring); S7 ladder (validation, API and page-run normalisation, ladder links as candidates, requests, pool, relevance, rung bands, link map, timeline, report, PDF, Data Table rows with exact columns, delivery, spawned payload round-trip, callback, store failure, all-pulls-failed stop); S8 tracker (plan, positions, history columns, second check with gains/drops, stop rule, no ladders); S9 WordPress payload; S10 site tracking (Search Console, GA4, inspection, trends, live checks, metrics, report; v4.6 trend and live-check reuse); S12 content cadence; S13 I published a page; S14 Console Alerts; S15 check-in; S16 business profile; S17 case study; S18 page types (hub, case study, local, video: brief context, schema, blog package, publish check); S19 AI visibility on real engine responses (incl. the weekly core-engine run with carried Gemini / Claude / brand); S20 backlinks on real data (full and light runs, quarterly gap reuse); S21 audit upgrades over two audits and the change-aware scheduler (never audited, 5 / 10 / 40 / 65 days, unchanged, changed, 404, undated index, on demand); S22 intake and Site Admin; v4.6 checks in S1 (editor gate), S5 (domain ages: cache, RDAP, WHOIS only for gaps) and S8 (unpublished pages monthly). Real DataForSEO responses captured on 2026-10-02 are in harness/fixtures_live/. Last run: 514 node runs, 0 failed. Generated reports land in harness/sample-output/; live samples are prefixed LIVE-.

10. Findings, fixes and roadmap

Found and fixed during this test

  • Credential slots for new nodes. The two ladder nodes created after the build's credential pass had none; the build now re-runs the pass and asserts every model / data node carries one.
  • Error workflow never fired. In n8n 2.x an error workflow must be published; SEO Agent — Error Handler had been imported unpublished. Published and verified with a deliberate failure.
  • Form completion pages end the execution. Branches still queued (discovery report/content/audit, the audit "started" page order) never ran. Follow-ups are now spawned as separate executions; completion nodes are last; the WordPress gate runs before delivery.
  • Tracker cost. $0.028 per keyword at depth 100 → depth 50 (~$0.015).
  • Ladder robustness. Coarse AI topic labels are split per page type and capped at 6 keywords; an empty research pool stops with a clear message.

Observations for tuning

  • Generated pages still overrun the length target (2,458 vs 1,800 words after one editor pass) — the same tendency as the earlier content test; consider a hard trim step in QA.
  • Discovery priorities depend on the seed source: the API run that read the site found "e invoicing" (1,900/mo), the form run with a business text found only low-volume finance terms; blend both seed sources.
  • The "e-invoicing uae" cluster landed in tier Later in the API discovery although it carried the highest opportunity; let opportunity override the goal-aware slot cap.
  • WHOIS was $0.48 of the $1.02 full-report spend; query RDAP first (done in v4.6, with a one-year cache).
  • When a ladder page run is rejected by a guard and the user left an e-mail but no callback, only ops is told; e-mail the user too.

Found and fixed on 2026-10-02 (v4.3-v4.6)

  • Text HTTP responses arrive under data, not body: the sitemap read as empty and the publish check would have read live pages as empty. All readers use body ?? data.
  • www vs bare host: the crawl reports www. URLs while canonicals and Google use the bare host; nothing counted as indexable and every inspection said "unknown to Google". URLs are compared host-agnostically and inspections use the canonical URL.
  • No URL constructor in the Code sandbox (a try/catch hid it): hosts are parsed with a regex; the harness shadows URL.
  • An AI Agent node outputs only {output}: the critic never saw the brief. Context is read with $('Node').
  • E-mail attachments: Send Email 2.1 ignores attachmentsUi; the blog package and the Site Tracker PDF were never attached. All e-mails use fileAttachments and retry 3× (transient EAI_AGAIN).
  • Link gap: domain_intersection needs intersection_mode: partial and carries the linking domain in target; the full report's gap was always empty.
  • Name-based lookups match other companies ("techand Dubai" → a construction firm): a Business Profile counts only with the site's website or phone; set explicit brand names.
  • The brand question inflated the AI mention rate (all first-run mentions came from it): rates use buyer questions only.
  • v4.6 reuse rules: a skipped audit must not overwrite the stored sitemap fingerprint; a failed rank check (-1) means check again, not "does not rank"; carried monthly results must still feed derived flags but not re-fire alerts.

Roadmap (REVIEW.md §6)

  1. Parallelise the full report (M7). Move Keywords, Backlinks, PageSpeed, Content Review and AI Visibility into sub-workflows started with Execute Workflow (no wait) and merge on completion; the wall-clock drops from ~20 to ~7 minutes and each module becomes independently testable and reusable through the API.
  2. Monthly cost roll-up: `run_ledger` is per run; a Data Table or sheet that sums it per customer/month lets you price with margin.
  3. Hosted report link: publish the PDF behind a signed URL so emails carry a link rather than a 200 KB attachment.
  4. Durable rate limiting and job tracking (M6): a Data Table keyed by execution id with status (accepted / running / done / failed), which also powers a `GET /status/:id` endpoint for API customers and a "check my report" page for form users.
  5. Prompt re-tune on Sonnet 5.5: the agents now run on Sonnet 5.5; after the first live runs, compare a few briefs/drafts against v3 outputs and adjust wording if needed.
  6. Quality evals for the content path: keep 10–20 real briefs/drafts, score them with a rubric (keyword placement, no invented facts, FAQ format, table present), and run the set whenever a prompt changes. Content QA already produces most of the signals.
  7. Apply the production overlay (`production/PRODUCTION.md`) before customer traffic, and move report email to a transactional sender on your domain.
  8. Competitor and cluster quality (M11, L3): SERP-overlap clustering and service-based seed queries make the discovery report noticeably sharper.
  9. Ladder follow-ups: e-mail the user (not only ops) when a ladder page run is rejected by the budget or rate guard; let the tracker start the next rung automatically when `ladder_auto_next_rung` is on (today recommend-only); treat unknown difficulty (KD null) explicitly in the rung bands; let discovery's tiering promote a very high-opportunity cluster into "Now" even when it is informational; use RDAP before DataForSEO WHOIS in the full report ($0.48 of the $1.02 run).
  10. Tracking follow-ups (see `TRACKING_FEATURE_SPEC.md`): Bing Webmaster Tools and Google Business Profile as further sources; charts in the PDF from the stored daily series and a hosted per-site dashboard reading `seo_site_metrics` / `seo_query_history`; feed Search Console striking-distance queries into discovery and ladder planning; auto-run the recommended improvement when `ladder_auto_next_rung` is on; prune `seo_query_history` after 12 months.
  11. E-E-A-T follow-ups (v4.4): one live content run per page role with a real profile; several authors per site (pick by topic) instead of one default author; an author page generator (bio, credentials, list of articles, Person schema) from the profile; Site Admin actions to delete a profile or a case study; a case-study prompt in the Monday report for sites with none; transcripts fetched automatically (YouTube Data API captions need OAuth — today the owner pastes them); local pages planned by the ladder for geo head terms.
  12. Monitor follow-ups (v4.5): AI-visibility trend charts from `seo_ai_visibility`; brand-name disambiguation (a short domain label like "techand" can match another company — set `brand_names` through Site Admin `monitors`); Google Business Profile through the official API (OAuth) for reviews and posts; a status page per prospect for outreach tracking; diff-aware fix pack (only what changed since the last audit); Bing / Copilot as an AI engine when DataForSEO offers it.
  13. Reuse follow-ups (v4.6): a full report or keyword run that reuses a recent technical audit (crawl + PageSpeed) for the same domain instead of crawling again; Search Console's `lastmod` and the IndexNow / sitemap ping on publish as further change signals for the Audit Scheduler; per-site cost roll-up from `run_ledger` to show what each reuse saved.

12. Appendix B: the page generated in the live test

The ladder's first page, "uae e invoicing penalties" (rung 1, Blog Post), exactly as the content pipeline delivered it on 2026-10-01 (execution 46): Opus brief and copy, Sonnet critique, deterministic QA, one Opus editor pass. Content score 85/100; text in [square brackets] is a placeholder the client must replace; the two internal links to the ladder's planned top page and sibling are part of the ladder design. JSON-LD blocks are omitted here (they are in the report files under harness/sample-output/LIVE-ladder-page-uae-e-invoicing-penalties.*).

Title: UAE E-Invoicing Penalties: Fines, Examples & Deadlines

UAE E-Invoicing Penalties: The Confirmed Fines, What They Could Cost and How to Avoid Them

Last updated: [Date] · Reviewed by [Name, UAE tax credential] · Technical review: [Reviewer name, role at Tech&]

UAE e-invoicing penalties are recurring monthly fines, so the cost of a late start accumulates every month. This guide gives your finance team four things: the confirmed fines in plain English, costed exposure for three scenarios, your phase deadline, and a prevention plan with an owner for each control. Where we have not confirmed the official text, we say so.

Quick answer: Under Cabinet Decision No. 106 of 2025, the Federal Tax Authority (FTA) can fine VAT-registered businesses that miss e-invoicing obligations. Failing to implement the Electronic Invoicing System or appoint an Accredited Service Provider costs [Price] per month or part month. Each late electronic invoice or credit note costs [Price] Reported wording sets an [Price] monthly cap for each document type. Voluntary adopters are not penalised.

What are the UAE e-invoicing penalties under Cabinet Decision No. 106 of 2025?

The Ministry of Finance published Cabinet Decision No. 106 of 2025 on e-invoicing violations and penalties in December 2025. It sits under Federal Decree-Law No. 28 of 2022 on Tax Procedures, and the FTA enforces it. Two administrative penalties are confirmed. A business with no e-invoicing system or ASP pays [Price] per month. Each late document costs [Price]

What is the penalty for not issuing an e-invoice?

Failing to issue and transmit an electronic invoice on time costs [Price] per invoice. Commentary on the decision reports a cap of [Price] per month. A PDF does not count. The invoice must pass through your ASP in the structured electronic format. If you have no system or ASP at all, the separate [Price] monthly penalty also applies.

The violations at a glance

Violation (paraphrased from the MoF decision)Plain-English meaningExampleWho is liableFineCap
Failure to implement the e-invoicing system, including appointing an ASP, by the deadlineYou are not connected through an accredited provider on timeYour mandatory date passes with the ASP contract unsignedBusiness in scope[Price] per month or part monthNone stated; recurs monthly
Failure to issue and transmit an electronic invoice on timeThe invoice does not go through your ASP in the structured electronic formatA batch of sales invoices goes out as PDFsIssuer[Price] per invoice[Price] per month (reported)
Failure to issue and transmit an electronic credit note on timeThe same rule, applied to credit notesMonth-end returns are credited manuallyIssuer[Price] per credit note[Price] per month (reported)
Failure to notify the FTA of a system failure within the set periodYou must tell the FTA when a fault stops you issuingAn ASP outage goes unreportedBusiness in scopeNot confirmed hereCheck the decision
Failure to notify the ASP of changes to your registered dataYour ASP's record of your business must stay currentA new branch is never notified to the ASPBusiness in scopeNot confirmed hereCheck the decision
Recipient failure to notify the issuer of a system failureA buyer must tell its supplier when it cannot receiveThe buyer's ASP is down and the supplier is not toldRecipient (as reported)Not confirmed hereCheck the decision

We have not confirmed the amounts, caps or notification windows for rows four to six. This guide therefore prices only the confirmed fines. Commentary groups the breaches into these six violations, so check the full list against the decision before using it in board papers.

The monthly fine applies for each month or part of a month. One day into a new month counts as a full month. The decision excludes voluntary adopters.

[Image: Infographic summarising the confirmed UAE e-invoicing penalties under Cabinet Decision No. 106 of 2025, with fines in AED]

Is e-invoicing mandatory in the UAE?

Yes. It is mandatory for VAT-registered businesses in scope, on a phased timetable set by Ministerial Decision No. 244 of 2025. Voluntary adoption opens from 1 July 2026. Businesses with revenue at or above [[Price] million](https://worldcompanysetup.com/en/blog/uae-e-invoicing-mandate-dubai-timeline-penalties/) must appoint an ASP first, by 31 July 2026. They must go live from 1 January 2027.

When do e-invoicing penalties start, and which phase are you in?

Penalties start only once your deadline passes. Ministerial Decision No. 244 of 2025 sets two dates for each phase: an ASP appointment deadline and a go-live date. Ministerial Decision No. 243 of 2025 defines scope and exclusions. Confirm the dates below against the official decision before planning.

PhaseAppoint an ASP byMandatory e-invoicing from
Revenue of [Price] million or more31 July 20261 January 2027
Revenue below [Price] million31 March 20271 July 2027
Government entities31 March 20271 October 2027

Published guides often disagree because they mix up these two dates. Your ASP deadline comes first, so it is your nearest exposure. A secondary guide to Ministerial Decision No. 244 explains the phases in more detail.

Are there penalties during the voluntary phase?

No. Cabinet Decision No. 106 of 2025 states that its provisions do not apply to businesses that issue, transmit or exchange e-invoices voluntarily. The safest time to test is therefore from 1 July 2026 until your mandatory date. Use that window to test your ERP, your ASP connection and your system-failure procedure without any fine exposure.

A three-question rule to find your deadline

  1. Are you VAT-registered and outside the exclusions in Ministerial Decision No. 243? If not, the mandate may not apply. Confirm this with your adviser.
  2. Is your revenue at or above [Price] million? If yes, the first-phase dates apply.
  3. Have you appointed an ASP? If not, your nearest exposure is the ASP deadline, not go-live.

Is the [Price] cap per violation or in total?

Reported wording of Cabinet Decision No. 106 sets a separate [Price] monthly cap for invoices and another for credit notes. The [Price] monthly system fine sits on top of both. The safe budgeting assumption is that penalties stack, giving a worst month of [Price] Confirm the clause with your adviser until the FTA clarifies it.

ReadingHow the cap worksWorst month, all three breaches
Per-category (stacking)[Price] cap for invoices, a separate [Price] for credit notes, plus the [Price] system fine[Price]
Overall capOne [Price] ceiling across all penalties in the month[Price]

Commentary describes the document fine as [capped at [Price] per month for each](https://www.genzone.com/uae-e-invoicing-2026-guide/) category, which supports the stacking reading. Read the clause in the MoF decision yourself.

How much could non-compliance cost? Worked penalty examples

A business with no ASP and regular late invoices faces up to [Price] a month. That is [Price] for the system plus the [Price] invoice cap. Add late credit notes and the worst month reaches [Price] Both figures use the stacking reading. The fines recur for each month or part of a month.

The exposure formula

> Monthly exposure = 5,000 (if no system or ASP) + min(late invoices × 100, 5,000) + min(late credit notes × 100, 5,000). All figures in AED per month, per-category reading. Under the overall-cap reading, the monthly total cannot exceed 5,000.

Three scenarios, three totals

ScenarioMonthsViolations each monthMonthly penalty AED (per-category)Total AED: per-categoryTotal AED: overall cap
A: enterprise above [Price]m6No ASP; 80 late invoices (8,000, capped to 5,000)5,000 + 5,000 = 10,00010,000 × 6 = 60,0005,000 × 6 = 30,000
B: system live, process gaps330 late invoices (3,000); 10 late credit notes (1,000)3,000 + 1,000 = 4,0004,000 × 3 = 12,0004,000 × 3 = 12,000

Scenario B is the same under both readings because its monthly total stays below [Price] Scenario C shows the full gap between the readings: [Price] over a year. The MoF text governs these figures, so take advice before you report them.

Costs that do not appear on the penalty notice

Remediation also consumes finance and IT time during close. For the wider analysis, see the full cost of e-invoicing non-compliance and mitigation.

What counts as a system failure, and what if your ASP goes down?

A system failure is a technical fault in your own system or your ASP's that stops you issuing or transmitting e-invoices. The decision requires you to notify the FTA within a set period and fines you if you do not. We have not confirmed that window or the amount, so take both from the MoF decision.

An ASP outage does not remove your own obligations. Do not assume your provider will notify the FTA for you. Check in the decision who must notify, then write that duty into your ASP contract.

System-failure incident checklist

  1. Detect and timestamp the failure, noting whether it sits in the ERP or the ASP.
  2. Raise a ticket with your ASP and record the reference.
  3. Notify the Federal Tax Authority within the required window and record the channel you used.
  4. Log the affected invoices, correspondence and screenshots, with detection, notification and resolution times.
  5. Re-transmit the backlog once the system failure is resolved, then reconcile.

What is an accredited service provider (ASP), and why does it matter for penalties?

An accredited service provider (ASP) is a provider accredited by the UAE authorities to exchange structured electronic invoices between businesses. It also reports invoice data to the FTA. Under UAE electronic invoicing, you cannot issue compliant e-invoices without an ASP. Missing your ASP appointment deadline triggers the [Price] monthly fine.

When you choose an ASP, check its accreditation status, PINT AE support, ERP connectors and uptime commitments. See the Peppol e-invoicing software features CFOs should look for.

Who is liable: the issuer, the recipient or the ASP?

As reported, the business carries the liability, not the service provider (ASP). Confirm the article for each violation in the MoF decision. UAE e-invoicing is commonly described as a five-corner model on Peppol and PINT AE. Data flows from the supplier to the supplier's ASP, then to the buyer's ASP and the buyer. The FTA is the fifth corner and receives the invoice data.

Issuers must issue and transmit each invoice and electronic credit note in the structured format on time. Recipients need an ASP and the ability to receive e-invoices. Commentary indicates they must also notify the issuer of failures. See what the FTA requires under UAE e-invoicing for the full obligations.

Your ASP is a service provider, so write SLAs, uptime targets and incident-reporting duties into the contract.

[Image: Five-corner Peppol model diagram showing issuer, recipient, Accredited Service Providers and the FTA]

How do e-invoicing fines relate to VAT penalties, and can you appeal?

They are separate schedules and can apply together. You can also request reconsideration.

Late VAT payment vs e-invoicing fines

E-invoicing fines sit in Cabinet Decision No. 106 of 2025. General VAT penalties, including those for late payment, sit under Cabinet Decision No. 40 of 2017 as amended. A late electronic invoice can therefore attract both an e-invoicing fine and a separate VAT penalty. Take current late-payment rates from the FTA's published penalty schedule.

Requesting reconsideration from the FTA

Federal Decree-Law No. 28 of 2022 lets you ask the FTA to reconsider a penalty within 40 business days of being notified. If you disagree with the outcome, you can object to the Tax Disputes Resolution Committee. Your incident logs and ASP correspondence are your evidence, so keep them from day one.

How to avoid UAE e-invoicing penalties: owners, controls and a 90-day plan

Each violation needs one owner and one documented control. Start with ASP selection, because [one compliance guide warns that delay risks bottlenecks and [Price] monthly fines](https://farahsolutions.ae/blog/uae-e-invoicing-guidelines-v1-1-compliance-guide).

Prevention matrix by owner

ViolationOwnerControlEvidence to keep
No system or ASPCFO (Finance)ASP contract signed before your ASP deadlineSigned contract
Late electronic invoiceIT/ERPAutomated transmission with a daily exception reportException reports
Late electronic credit noteFinance (AR)Credit-note workflow routed through the ASPApproval trail
No FTA failure noticeFinanceIncident procedure with a named notifierIncident log
ASP data not updatedFinanceQuarterly master-data reviewChange confirmations
Recipient fails to notify issuerASP and Finance (AP)Contractual alerting SLAASP alerts

A 90-day penalty-avoidance plan

  1. Days 1–30: confirm your phase, scope your entities, shortlist ASPs and run an ERP gap check for PINT AE fields.
  2. Days 31–60: appoint the ASP, map invoice data and configure credit-note flows.
  3. Days 61–90: test end to end during the voluntary window, when no penalties apply. Rehearse the system-failure procedure and train your AP and AR teams.

Dynamics 365 Finance & Operations and Business Central both need ERP-side changes. See upgrading your ERP for Peppol PINT AE compliance.

Frequently Asked Questions

Q1: Is e-invoicing mandatory in the UAE?

A1: Yes, for VAT-registered businesses in scope, on a phased timetable under Ministerial Decision No. 244 of 2025. Voluntary adoption opens from 1 July 2026. Businesses with revenue of [Price] million or more must appoint an ASP by 31 July 2026 and go live from 1 January 2027. Our complete guide to e-invoicing in the UAE covers scope.

Q2: What is the penalty for not issuing an e-invoice?

A2: Failing to issue and transmit an electronic invoice on time carries a penalty of [Price] per invoice, capped at [Price] per month according to published commentary. If you have not implemented the system or appointed an ASP at all, a separate [Price] monthly penalty can apply as well.

Q3: Who is exempted from e-invoicing?

A3: Ministerial Decision No. 243 of 2025 lists the excluded transactions and entities. Being VAT-registered does not by itself put every transaction in scope. Check the official exclusion list against each of your revenue streams. Confirm any borderline cases with your tax adviser before your phase begins, because exclusions apply to specific transactions rather than to whole businesses by default.

Q4: What is the penalty for late payment of VAT in the UAE?

A4: Late VAT payment penalties fall under Cabinet Decision No. 40 of 2017 as amended, not under the e-invoicing decision. Rates have changed over time, so take the current figures from the Federal Tax Authority's published penalty schedule. Both penalty regimes can apply to the same business in the same period.

Q5: Are there penalties during the voluntary phase?

A5: No. Cabinet Decision No. 106 of 2025 states that its provisions do not apply to businesses that issue, transmit or exchange e-invoices voluntarily. This makes the voluntary window the safest time to test your ERP and ASP connection before your mandatory date.

Q6: What penalties apply to notification failures?

A6: The decision covers three notification duties. You must tell the FTA about a system failure and tell your ASP about changes to your registered data. Recipients must tell issuers when they cannot receive. We have not confirmed the amounts or time windows, so read the exact clauses in the MoF decision before you budget for them.

Q7: Do e-invoicing penalties apply to credit notes?

A7: Yes. Each electronic credit note not issued and transmitted on time costs [Price] capped at [Price] per month (source). Reported wording suggests this cap sits separately from the invoice cap. Late credit notes could therefore add to invoice penalties in the same month.

Q8: Where can I download the UAE e-invoicing penalties PDF?

A8: The official text is the Ministry of Finance's Cabinet Decision on e-invoicing violations and penalties, published as a PDF. The violations table on this page summarises it in plain English. Check the decision itself for any amounts we mark as not confirmed.

Opportunity = traffic potential (volume after SERP-feature click loss) × winnability (difficulty and top-10 link strength) × commercial value (CPC) × intent weight for your goal × trend, then multiplied by AI relevance (2 = core, 1 = adjacent, 0 = dropped).

1. Executive Summary

We crawled 88 pages of techand.ai and ran additional checks on security, caching, crawler access and the sitemap. We found:

CriticalHighMediumLowInfo
136103
Score limit applied: 1 critical issue found — score capped at 70 until fixed. Calculated score before the limit: 81/100.

Fix These First

  1. Broken pages (4xx, 5xx or timeout) (3 of 88 crawled pages)
    Fix or 301-redirect broken URLs to the most relevant live page.
  2. www and non-www versions are not consolidated (Crawler canonicalization test failed)
    Make the www version redirect (301) to the main domain with a valid certificate for both hostnames.
  3. HTML cannot be cached by the CDN (no-store / private) (Cache-Control: private, no-cache, no-store, max-age=0, must-revalidate; CDN cache status on two requests: MISS / MISS)
    Remove the setting that forces dynamic rendering (for Next.js: force-dynamic, or cookies()/headers() in server components) or enable revalidation so the CDN can cache HTML. Verify: repeat requests should show a cache HIT.
  4. Broken internal links (21 broken links across 88 pages)
    Export the broken links from the crawl and fix or remove each one; redirect deleted pages to the closest live page.

4. Issues Found

Critical Priority (1)

Critical Broken pages (4xx, 5xx or timeout)
Crawlability & Indexing  •  3 of 88 pages (3%)

Evidence: 3 of 88 crawled pages

Why it matters: Pages that return errors waste crawl budget and lose any ranking signals pointing to them.

How to fix: Fix or 301-redirect broken URLs to the most relevant live page.

Affected pages:
https://www.techand.ai/capabilities/dynamics-365-project-operations-uae
https://www.techand.ai/insights/dynamics-365-project-operations-uae
https://www.techand.ai/whatwedo/dynamics-365-project-operations-uae

High Priority (3)

High www and non-www versions are not consolidated
Crawlability & Indexing  •  Whole website

Evidence: Crawler canonicalization test failed

Why it matters: If both versions do not resolve to one canonical host, links and signals are split.

How to fix: Make the www version redirect (301) to the main domain with a valid certificate for both hostnames.

High HTML cannot be cached by the CDN (no-store / private)
Performance  •  Whole website

Evidence: Cache-Control: private, no-cache, no-store, max-age=0, must-revalidate; CDN cache status on two requests: MISS / MISS

Why it matters: Every visit forces the server to rebuild the page from scratch, which slows the first byte for every user and hurts Core Web Vitals. This is sometimes intentional (carts, logged-in areas) — confirm before changing.

How to fix: Remove the setting that forces dynamic rendering (for Next.js: force-dynamic, or cookies()/headers() in server components) or enable revalidation so the CDN can cache HTML. Verify: repeat requests should show a cache HIT.

High Broken internal links
Crawlability & Indexing  •  Whole website

Evidence: 21 broken links across 88 pages

Why it matters: Links to pages that no longer exist waste crawl budget and send users to dead ends.

How to fix: Export the broken links from the crawl and fix or remove each one; redirect deleted pages to the closest live page.

Medium Priority (6)

Medium Missing canonical tag
Crawlability & Indexing  •  45 of 88 pages (51%)

Evidence: 45 of 88 crawled pages

Why it matters: Without a canonical, duplicate URL versions can compete with each other.

How to fix: Add a self-referencing canonical tag to every indexable page.

Medium Large image payload (over 1MB of images per page)
Performance  •  33 of 88 pages (38%)

Evidence: 33 of 88 crawled pages

Why it matters: Large images are the most common cause of slow mobile pages. The figure is the total size of image files the page references (including responsive sizes), so the real download may be smaller, but it signals unoptimised images.

How to fix: Compress images, serve WebP/AVIF, and make sure image CDN optimisation (e.g. f_auto,q_auto) is on.

5. Action Plan

Phase 1 — Urgent (this week)

  • Broken pages (4xx, 5xx or timeout)
  • www and non-www versions are not consolidated
  • HTML cannot be cached by the CDN (no-store / private)
  • Broken internal links

Phase 2 — Important (next 2-4 weeks)

  • Missing canonical tag
  • Canonical points to a redirect
  • No compression (gzip/brotli)
  • Large image payload (over 1MB of images per page)
  • Structured data with errors
  • Brand name repeated in the title

Phase 3 — Polish (when time allows)

  • Missing security headers
  • Missing Open Graph tags
  • URLs are not SEO-friendly
  • Internal URLs that redirect
  • Links pointing to redirects
  • Articles with no outbound links to sources
  • Title shorter than 30 characters
  • Long or messy URLs
  • Few internal links pointing to the page
  • Light content (300-500 words)
  • Missing Twitter/X card tags
  • Canonical points to a different page
  • H1 is identical to the title tag

6. Not Assessed in This Audit

This is a technical site audit. The following areas are part of the Full SEO Report and are not included in this score:

  • Structured data (schema) and HTML-level checks
  • Competitor benchmark (organic keywords, rankings, traffic, domain age)
  • Core Web Vitals (LCP, CLS, INP) from Google PageSpeed and real users
  • Content quality and E-E-A-T review (authors, case studies, claims, pricing)
  • Backlinks and referring domains
  • Keyword rankings by search intent and keyword gap
  • AI answer visibility (AI Overviews, ChatGPT brand mentions)

7. Methodology

Technical site health only. Each category starts at 100. Findings written by the AI content review, and notes about data that could not be collected, are listed but do not change the score. Each other finding deducts Critical 30, High 15, Medium 7, Low 3, Info 1 points, scaled by the share of pages affected (60%-100% of the penalty); site-wide issues take the full penalty. Within a category, each further finding of the same severity counts 70% of the previous one, so many small issues cannot zero a category on their own. The score is the weighted average of category scores, capped at 70 with one critical issue, 60 with two or more, and 85 with five or more high-priority issues.

SeverityPoints deducted
Critical30
High15
Medium7
Low3
Info1

Data Sources

SourceUsed for
Full site crawl (DataForSEO On-Page)Page-level checks on up to 200 pages, including sitemap URLs; duplicate tags, non-indexable pages and redirect chains reports
Direct HTTP checkswww/HTTPS, caching, security headers, robots.txt, sitemap, llms.txt
Crawler simulationAccess for 11 search and AI crawlers (user-agent based)

AI crawler checks use the crawler user-agent only; real crawlers are also verified by IP, so a pass here means no user-agent based blocking.

Some extra crawl reports were unavailable: duplicate_title: Task Not Found.; duplicate_description: Task Not Found.; non_indexable: Task Not Found.; redirect_chains: Task Not Found.

1. Executive Summary

About the site: Tech& is a Dubai-based (DAFZA) Microsoft Dynamics 365, Business Central, Power Platform and AI/Copilot consultancy serving GCC enterprises and banks, the public sector, retail, hospitality and other industries.

The main thing holding this site back: The site makes broad capability and scale claims but gives no verifiable proof, such as named experts, Microsoft credentials, named client case studies or reviews, so buyers have little reason to trust it.

Fix These First

  1. Broken pages (4xx, 5xx or timeout) (3 of 88 crawled pages)
    Fix or 301-redirect broken URLs to the most relevant live page.
  2. www and non-www versions are not consolidated (Crawler canonicalization test failed)
    Make the www version redirect (301) to the main domain with a valid certificate for both hostnames.
  3. HTML cannot be cached by the CDN (no-store / private) (Cache-Control: private, no-cache, no-store, max-age=0, must-revalidate; CDN cache status on two requests: MISS / MISS)
    Remove the setting that forces dynamic rendering (for Next.js: force-dynamic, or cookies()/headers() in server components) or enable revalidation so the CDN can cache HTML. Verify: repeat requests should show a cache HIT.
  4. Malformed sameAs profile link (Invalid: https://twitter.com/Tech&)
    Remove or correct the invalid entry and link only real, active profiles.
  5. Structured contact details are thinner than what the site shows (Missing in schema: streetAddress, addressLocality, telephone, email)
    Add full PostalAddress (streetAddress, addressLocality, addressRegion, postalCode, addressCountry), telephone and email to Organization schema.
  6. Experience claim conflicts with the founding date ("20+ years" on https://www.techand.ai/ but schema foundingDate is 2020)
    Make the claims consistent (e.g. "leadership with 20+ years of experience") or correct the founding date.
  7. Service pages have no Service schema (5 of 5 sampled service pages)
    Add Service JSON-LD (serviceType, provider → Organization @id, areaServed) on each service page.

Biggest Speed Savings (your homepage)

  • Avoid multiple page redirects — about 780ms

Findings

High HTML cannot be cached by the CDN (no-store / private)
Whole website

Evidence: Cache-Control: private, no-cache, no-store, max-age=0, must-revalidate; CDN cache status on two requests: MISS / MISS

Why it matters: Every visit forces the server to rebuild the page from scratch, which slows the first byte for every user and hurts Core Web Vitals. This is sometimes intentional (carts, logged-in areas) — confirm before changing.

How to fix: Remove the setting that forces dynamic rendering (for Next.js: force-dynamic, or cookies()/headers() in server components) or enable revalidation so the CDN can cache HTML. Verify: repeat requests should show a cache HIT.

Medium Largest Contentful Paint needs improvement (2.5-4s)
Whole website

Evidence: 3481ms (lab test, mobile)

Why it matters: Google rates LCP above 2.5 seconds as Needs Improvement.

How to fix: Speed up server response and optimise the main image.

Affected pages:
https://techand.ai/

Medium No compression (gzip/brotli)
42 of 88 pages (48%)

Evidence: 42 of 88 crawled pages

Why it matters: Uncompressed pages take longer to download.

How to fix: Enable gzip or brotli compression on the server.

Medium Large image payload (over 1MB of images per page)
33 of 88 pages (38%)

Evidence: 33 of 88 crawled pages

Why it matters: Large images are the most common cause of slow mobile pages. The figure is the total size of image files the page references (including responsive sizes), so the real download may be smaller, but it signals unoptimised images.

How to fix: Compress images, serve WebP/AVIF, and make sure image CDN optimisation (e.g. f_auto,q_auto) is on.

High ChatGPT recommends other providers, not you
Whole website

Evidence: For "I need to choose a provider. Which are the best ai in cloud company United Arab Emirates? Give me up to 8 companies with…" ChatGPT listed: nxaara.com, nexait.ai, oracle.com, ae.scc.com, cloud.netcity.ae, themiddleeast.cloud

Why it matters: Buyers increasingly ask AI assistants for shortlists; not being named means not being considered.

How to fix: Get listed where AI answers draw their sources: the directories and review sites cited (nxaara.com, nexait.ai, oracle.com, ae.scc.com), plus comparison and "best X" pages that name you.

9. Structured Data (Schema)

Score: 47/100

High Malformed sameAs profile link
Whole website

Evidence: Invalid: https://twitter.com/Tech&

Why it matters: A broken identity link is worse than none: it confuses entity matching in Google and AI systems.

How to fix: Remove or correct the invalid entry and link only real, active profiles.

High Structured contact details are thinner than what the site shows
Whole website

Evidence: Missing in schema: streetAddress, addressLocality, telephone, email

Why it matters: Search engines cannot match your business listing to your site when the address and phone in schema are incomplete.

How to fix: Add full PostalAddress (streetAddress, addressLocality, addressRegion, postalCode, addressCountry), telephone and email to Organization schema.

High Service pages have no Service schema
5 of 88 pages (100%)

Evidence: 5 of 5 sampled service pages

Why it matters: Service schema tells search engines exactly what you offer, where and by whom, and separates similar service pages.

How to fix: Add Service JSON-LD (serviceType, provider → Organization @id, areaServed) on each service page.

Affected pages:

https://www.techand.ai/whatwedo/managed-services

High www and non-www versions are not consolidated
Whole website

Evidence: Crawler canonicalization test failed

Why it matters: If both versions do not resolve to one canonical host, links and signals are split.

How to fix: Make the www version redirect (301) to the main domain with a valid certificate for both hostnames.

High Far fewer pages indexed than crawled
Whole website

Evidence: Google site: estimate 5 vs 88 crawlable pages

Why it matters: Pages that are not indexed cannot rank. This is an estimate; confirm in Search Console.

How to fix: Check Search Console → Pages for "Crawled – currently not indexed" and "Discovered – currently not indexed", improve internal links and content depth on those URLs.

High Broken internal links
Whole website

Evidence: 21 broken links across 88 pages

Why it matters: Links to pages that no longer exist waste crawl budget and send users to dead ends.

How to fix: Export the broken links from the crawl and fix or remove each one; redirect deleted pages to the closest live page.

13. What Works Well

  • Valid SSL certificate on the main domain (expires 2026-12-29)
  • HTTP correctly redirects to HTTPS
  • robots.txt is present
  • HTTP/2 is enabled
  • Missing pages return a proper 404
  • No broken images, scripts or stylesheets
  • No duplicate page titles
  • No duplicate meta descriptions
  • No duplicate content across pages
  • No redirect loops
  • All crawled pages are indexable
  • Every page has an H1
  • Every page has a title tag
  • One canonical host: https://techand.ai 301-redirects to https://www.techand.ai
  • HTTP redirects to HTTPS
  • robots.txt lists the sitemap
  • XML sitemap lists 43 URLs
  • Every live page is listed in the sitemap
  • llms.txt exists (10296 characters)
  • Site serves Markdown to AI agents on request
  • All 11 search and AI crawlers tested (GPTBot, ClaudeBot, PerplexityBot, Googlebot and more) receive the full page
  • Structured data present on the homepage (Organization)
  • Organization schema found (Organization)
  • Sampled articles link to external sources
  • Stable layout (CLS 0.00)
  • Clear contact details: a UAE phone number, a DAFZA Dubai address, an email and business hours (UTC+4).
  • Strong UAE and GCC positioning, with UAE-specific service naming and industry pages including banking and public sector.
  • Timely, relevant insight content such as Peppol e-invoicing for UAE CFOs and D365 reporting, with a table of contents and read time.
  • A broad, well-organised Microsoft stack offering covering D365 F&O, Business Central, Power Platform and Copilot/AI.
  • ChatGPT (with web search) correctly identifies the company

14. Action Plan

Phase 1 — Fix the foundations (Week 1)

  • Broken pages (4xx, 5xx or timeout) [Critical]
  • www and non-www versions are not consolidated [High]
  • HTML cannot be cached by the CDN (no-store / private) [High]
  • Malformed sameAs profile link [High]
  • Structured contact details are thinner than what the site shows [High]
  • Service pages have no Service schema [High]
  • No BreadcrumbList schema [High]
  • Far fewer pages indexed than crawled [High]
  • Broken internal links [High]

Phase 2 — Content and on-page (Weeks 2-5)

  • Experience claim conflicts with the founding date [High]
  • Unproven scale and financial claims on contact page [High]
  • No named authors or expert bylines on insight articles [High]
  • Partner status and certifications not evidenced [High]
  • Case study and testimonial proof is thin [High]
  • Articles have no named human author [High]
  • Few verified profiles linked in sameAs [Medium]
  • No freshness signal on articles [Medium]
  • Images without width and height [Medium]
  • Service pages are generic and duplicated [Medium]
  • Heavy repeated mega-menu text on every page [Medium]
  • Formatting artifact and only high-commitment CTAs [Medium]
  • No pricing or engagement-model guidance, and no leadership info [Medium]
  • Structured data with errors [Medium]
  • Brand name repeated in the title [Medium]

Phase 3 — Authority, keywords and AI visibility (run in parallel) (Weeks 2-12)

  • The site does not rank in Google’s top 10 for any keyword [High]
  • Organic traffic is under 10% of competitors [High]
  • Very young domain competing with long-established sites [High]
  • Keyword gap: searches competitors win and you do not [High]
  • Far fewer referring domains than competitors [High]
  • ChatGPT recommends other providers, not you [High]
  • Perplexity recommends other providers, not you [High]
  • Site does not rank at the top for its own brand name [High]
  • No rankings for informational searches [Medium]
  • High backlink spam score [Medium]
  • Google shows AI Overviews for your key searches and does not cite you [Medium]
  • Low-difficulty keywords you can target now [Low]

Phase 4 — Polish (Months 2-3)

  • Contact form is not in the server-rendered HTML [Medium]
  • Largest Contentful Paint needs improvement (2.5-4s) [Medium]
  • Missing canonical tag [Medium]
  • Canonical points to a redirect [Medium]
  • No compression (gzip/brotli) [Medium]
  • Large image payload (over 1MB of images per page) [Medium]
  • Missing security headers [Low]
  • No alternateName in Organization schema [Low]
  • Organization schema has no @id [Low]
  • Homepage H1 does not name the business or what it does [Low]
  • Phone number in a link but not shown on the page [Low]
  • No speculation rules for instant navigation [Low]
  • Top speed-up opportunities [Low]
  • Missing Open Graph tags [Low]
  • URLs are not SEO-friendly [Low]
  • Internal URLs that redirect [Low]
  • Links pointing to redirects [Low]
  • Title shorter than 30 characters [Low]
  • Long or messy URLs [Low]
  • Few internal links pointing to the page [Low]
  • Light content (300-500 words) [Low]

15. Leading Indicators to Track

  • SEO Health Score on the next audit (now 64/100)
  • Homepage CDN cache status shows HIT and server response under 200ms
  • Keywords in Google top 10 (now 0, competitor median 381)
  • Rankings for the easy-win keywords listed in section 4
  • Referring domains (now 13, competitor median 8,861.5)
  • Mobile PageSpeed score (now 89)
  • Whether ChatGPT and Perplexity name you for category queries, and whether Google AI Overviews cite you
  • Position for your brand name in Google

16. Methodology & Data Sources

Full SEO health. Each category starts at 100. Findings written by the AI content review, and notes about data that could not be collected, are listed but do not change the score. Each other finding deducts Critical 30, High 15, Medium 7, Low 3, Info 1 points, scaled by the share of pages affected (60%-100% of the penalty); site-wide issues take the full penalty. Within a category, each further finding of the same severity counts 70% of the previous one, so many small issues cannot zero a category on their own. The score is the weighted average of category scores, capped at 70 with one critical issue, 60 with two or more, and 85 with five or more high-priority issues.

SeverityPoints deducted
Critical30
High15
Medium7
Low3
Info1
SourceUsed for

DataForSEO LabsCompetitors, rankings, traffic estimates, keyword ideas by intent, keyword gap
DataForSEO BacklinksBacklinks, referring domains, link gap
Google PageSpeed InsightsLighthouse lab data and real-user Core Web Vitals (mobile)
Google search results (live)Brand and category rankings, AI Overview citations, competitor discovery
AI content review (Claude)Content quality and E-E-A-T review of sampled pages (listed, not scored)
DataForSEO LLM ResponsesLive ChatGPT (web search) and Perplexity answers about the brand and category
DataForSEO On-Page reportsDuplicate titles/descriptions, non-indexable pages, redirect chains
Domain registry dataDomain registration date

AI crawler checks use the crawler user-agent only; real crawlers are also verified by IP, so a pass here means no user-agent based blocking.

2. Is the destination realistic?

Google ranks the head term for the site with the most complete, trusted coverage of the topic. The ladder builds that coverage from the bottom: specific pages that are winnable now, linking up to one strong page for the head term.

Why

  • Volume of 880/month with a CPC of $6.85 shows real commercial value. The mandate is close: Wave 1 must appoint an ASP by 30 Oct 2026 and go live on 1 Jan 2027, and Wave 1 is exactly Tech&'s enterprise audience (revenue ≥ AED 50m).
  • Keyword difficulty is 2 (Easy) and competitors average only 5 referring domains and 10 backlinks. Link barriers are low, even though the site's authority is unknown.
  • The content gaps are clear. All six ranking pages have 0 tables, and none offers a wave-finder, an ASP selection scorecard or a backwards-planned onboarding timeline. A structured page with a lead path can beat ClearTax (2,628 words) and EDICOM (1,888 words), and MoF (713 words, no lead path) is a weak competitor on conversion.
  • The SERP is informational and mixed. It has government, Big-4 and vendor pages, an AI Overview and no featured snippet. A pure service page would mismatch that intent, so the page needs to be a guide-style service page.

What must change first

  • Build a hybrid service page: open with a 40-70 word answer on what e-invoicing is and the mandatory dates, then give the detailed guide content, then the Dynamics 365 / Microsoft e-invoicing service offer.
  • Add a Wave 1 vs Wave 2 wave-finder table, a backwards-planned timeline from 30 Oct 2026 and 31 Mar 2027, and an ASP selection scorecard labelled as Tech&'s own framework.
  • Add question-style H2s and FAQ schema matching the People Also Ask questions, plus a sample invoice field table and a readiness checklist, with a free readiness assessment CTA.
  • Add trust signals: a named expert author, a last-updated date, citations to MoF, Ministerial Decisions 243, 244 and 64, and Cabinet Decision 106, ASP partner or accreditation status, a UAE contact, and case studies.
  • Support the page with spoke content on deadline, requirements, ASP list and ERP readiness (D365, SAP), and build internal links and a few UAE finance backlinks.
Expected visits/month at #1-3300
Time to rank (verdict estimate)4 months
