UAE E-Invoicing Penalties: The Confirmed Fines, What They Could Cost and How to Avoid Them

Last updated: [Date] · Reviewed by [Name, UAE tax credential] · Technical review: [Reviewer name, role at Tech&]

UAE e-invoicing penalties are recurring monthly fines, so the cost of a late start accumulates every month. This guide gives your finance team four things: the confirmed fines in plain English, costed exposure for three scenarios, your phase deadline, and a prevention plan with an owner for each control. Where we have not confirmed the official text, we say so.

Quick answer: Under Cabinet Decision No. 106 of 2025, the Federal Tax Authority (FTA) can fine VAT-registered businesses that miss e-invoicing obligations. Failing to implement the Electronic Invoicing System or appoint an Accredited Service Provider costs [Price] per month or part month. Each late electronic invoice or credit note costs [Price] Reported wording sets an [Price] monthly cap for each document type. Voluntary adopters are not penalised.

What are the UAE e-invoicing penalties under Cabinet Decision No. 106 of 2025?

The Ministry of Finance published Cabinet Decision No. 106 of 2025 on e-invoicing violations and penalties in December 2025. It sits under Federal Decree-Law No. 28 of 2022 on Tax Procedures, and the FTA enforces it. Two administrative penalties are confirmed. A business with no e-invoicing system or ASP pays [Price] per month. Each late document costs [Price]

What is the penalty for not issuing an e-invoice?

Failing to issue and transmit an electronic invoice on time costs [Price] per invoice. Commentary on the decision reports a cap of [Price] per month. A PDF does not count. The invoice must pass through your ASP in the structured electronic format. If you have no system or ASP at all, the separate [Price] monthly penalty also applies.

The violations at a glance

Violation (paraphrased from the MoF decision)Plain-English meaningExampleWho is liableFineCap
Failure to implement the e-invoicing system, including appointing an ASP, by the deadlineYou are not connected through an accredited provider on timeYour mandatory date passes with the ASP contract unsignedBusiness in scope[Price] per month or part monthNone stated; recurs monthly
Failure to issue and transmit an electronic invoice on timeThe invoice does not go through your ASP in the structured electronic formatA batch of sales invoices goes out as PDFsIssuer[Price] per invoice[Price] per month (reported)
Failure to issue and transmit an electronic credit note on timeThe same rule, applied to credit notesMonth-end returns are credited manuallyIssuer[Price] per credit note[Price] per month (reported)
Failure to notify the FTA of a system failure within the set periodYou must tell the FTA when a fault stops you issuingAn ASP outage goes unreportedBusiness in scopeNot confirmed hereCheck the decision
Failure to notify the ASP of changes to your registered dataYour ASP's record of your business must stay currentA new branch is never notified to the ASPBusiness in scopeNot confirmed hereCheck the decision
Recipient failure to notify the issuer of a system failureA buyer must tell its supplier when it cannot receiveThe buyer's ASP is down and the supplier is not toldRecipient (as reported)Not confirmed hereCheck the decision

We have not confirmed the amounts, caps or notification windows for rows four to six. This guide therefore prices only the confirmed fines. Commentary groups the breaches into these six violations, so check the full list against the decision before using it in board papers.

The monthly fine applies for each month or part of a month. One day into a new month counts as a full month. The decision excludes voluntary adopters.

[Image: Infographic summarising the confirmed UAE e-invoicing penalties under Cabinet Decision No. 106 of 2025, with fines in AED]

Is e-invoicing mandatory in the UAE?

Yes. It is mandatory for VAT-registered businesses in scope, on a phased timetable set by Ministerial Decision No. 244 of 2025. Voluntary adoption opens from 1 July 2026. Businesses with revenue at or above [[Price] million](https://worldcompanysetup.com/en/blog/uae-e-invoicing-mandate-dubai-timeline-penalties/) must appoint an ASP first, by 31 July 2026. They must go live from 1 January 2027.

When do e-invoicing penalties start, and which phase are you in?

Penalties start only once your deadline passes. Ministerial Decision No. 244 of 2025 sets two dates for each phase: an ASP appointment deadline and a go-live date. Ministerial Decision No. 243 of 2025 defines scope and exclusions. Confirm the dates below against the official decision before planning.

PhaseAppoint an ASP byMandatory e-invoicing from
Revenue of [Price] million or more31 July 20261 January 2027
Revenue below [Price] million31 March 20271 July 2027
Government entities31 March 20271 October 2027

Published guides often disagree because they mix up these two dates. Your ASP deadline comes first, so it is your nearest exposure. A secondary guide to Ministerial Decision No. 244 explains the phases in more detail.

Are there penalties during the voluntary phase?

No. Cabinet Decision No. 106 of 2025 states that its provisions do not apply to businesses that issue, transmit or exchange e-invoices voluntarily. The safest time to test is therefore from 1 July 2026 until your mandatory date. Use that window to test your ERP, your ASP connection and your system-failure procedure without any fine exposure.

A three-question rule to find your deadline

  1. Are you VAT-registered and outside the exclusions in Ministerial Decision No. 243? If not, the mandate may not apply. Confirm this with your adviser.
  2. Is your revenue at or above [Price] million? If yes, the first-phase dates apply.
  3. Have you appointed an ASP? If not, your nearest exposure is the ASP deadline, not go-live.

Is the [Price] cap per violation or in total?

Reported wording of Cabinet Decision No. 106 sets a separate [Price] monthly cap for invoices and another for credit notes. The [Price] monthly system fine sits on top of both. The safe budgeting assumption is that penalties stack, giving a worst month of [Price] Confirm the clause with your adviser until the FTA clarifies it.

ReadingHow the cap worksWorst month, all three breaches
Per-category (stacking)[Price] cap for invoices, a separate [Price] for credit notes, plus the [Price] system fine[Price]
Overall capOne [Price] ceiling across all penalties in the month[Price]

Commentary describes the document fine as [capped at [Price] per month for each](https://www.genzone.com/uae-e-invoicing-2026-guide/) category, which supports the stacking reading. Read the clause in the MoF decision yourself.

How much could non-compliance cost? Worked penalty examples

A business with no ASP and regular late invoices faces up to [Price] a month. That is [Price] for the system plus the [Price] invoice cap. Add late credit notes and the worst month reaches [Price] Both figures use the stacking reading. The fines recur for each month or part of a month.

The exposure formula

Monthly exposure = 5,000 (if no system or ASP) + min(late invoices × 100, 5,000) + min(late credit notes × 100, 5,000). All figures in AED per month, per-category reading. Under the overall-cap reading, the monthly total cannot exceed 5,000.

Three scenarios, three totals

UAE e-invoicing penalty exposure: three worked scenarios

ScenarioMonthsViolations each monthMonthly penalty AED (per-category)Total AED: per-categoryTotal AED: overall cap
A: enterprise above [Price]m6No ASP; 80 late invoices (8,000, capped to 5,000)5,000 + 5,000 = 10,00010,000 × 6 = 60,0005,000 × 6 = 30,000
B: system live, process gaps330 late invoices (3,000); 10 late credit notes (1,000)3,000 + 1,000 = 4,0004,000 × 3 = 12,0004,000 × 3 = 12,000
C: no system at all12No ASP; 60 late invoices (6,000, capped to 5,000); 55 late credit notes (5,500, capped to 5,000)5,000 + 5,000 + 5,000 = 15,00015,000 × 12 = 180,0005,000 × 12 = 60,000

Scenario B is the same under both readings because its monthly total stays below [Price] Scenario C shows the full gap between the readings: [Price] over a year. The MoF text governs these figures, so take advice before you report them.

Costs that do not appear on the penalty notice

Remediation also consumes finance and IT time during close. For the wider analysis, see the full cost of e-invoicing non-compliance and mitigation.

What counts as a system failure, and what if your ASP goes down?

A system failure is a technical fault in your own system or your ASP's that stops you issuing or transmitting e-invoices. The decision requires you to notify the FTA within a set period and fines you if you do not. We have not confirmed that window or the amount, so take both from the MoF decision.

An ASP outage does not remove your own obligations. Do not assume your provider will notify the FTA for you. Check in the decision who must notify, then write that duty into your ASP contract.

System-failure incident checklist

  1. Detect and timestamp the failure, noting whether it sits in the ERP or the ASP.
  2. Raise a ticket with your ASP and record the reference.
  3. Notify the Federal Tax Authority within the required window and record the channel you used.
  4. Log the affected invoices, correspondence and screenshots, with detection, notification and resolution times.
  5. Re-transmit the backlog once the system failure is resolved, then reconcile.

What is an accredited service provider (ASP), and why does it matter for penalties?

An accredited service provider (ASP) is a provider accredited by the UAE authorities to exchange structured electronic invoices between businesses. It also reports invoice data to the FTA. Under UAE electronic invoicing, you cannot issue compliant e-invoices without an ASP. Missing your ASP appointment deadline triggers the [Price] monthly fine.

When you choose an ASP, check its accreditation status, PINT AE support, ERP connectors and uptime commitments. See the Peppol e-invoicing software features CFOs should look for.

Who is liable: the issuer, the recipient or the ASP?

As reported, the business carries the liability, not the service provider (ASP). Confirm the article for each violation in the MoF decision. UAE e-invoicing is commonly described as a five-corner model on Peppol and PINT AE. Data flows from the supplier to the supplier's ASP, then to the buyer's ASP and the buyer. The FTA is the fifth corner and receives the invoice data.

Issuers must issue and transmit each invoice and electronic credit note in the structured format on time. Recipients need an ASP and the ability to receive e-invoices. Commentary indicates they must also notify the issuer of failures. See what the FTA requires under UAE e-invoicing for the full obligations.

Your ASP is a service provider, so write SLAs, uptime targets and incident-reporting duties into the contract.

[Image: Five-corner Peppol model diagram showing issuer, recipient, Accredited Service Providers and the FTA]

How do e-invoicing fines relate to VAT penalties, and can you appeal?

They are separate schedules and can apply together. You can also request reconsideration.

Late VAT payment vs e-invoicing fines

E-invoicing fines sit in Cabinet Decision No. 106 of 2025. General VAT penalties, including those for late payment, sit under Cabinet Decision No. 40 of 2017 as amended. A late electronic invoice can therefore attract both an e-invoicing fine and a separate VAT penalty. Take current late-payment rates from the FTA's published penalty schedule.

Requesting reconsideration from the FTA

Federal Decree-Law No. 28 of 2022 lets you ask the FTA to reconsider a penalty within 40 business days of being notified. If you disagree with the outcome, you can object to the Tax Disputes Resolution Committee. Your incident logs and ASP correspondence are your evidence, so keep them from day one.

How to avoid UAE e-invoicing penalties: owners, controls and a 90-day plan

Each violation needs one owner and one documented control. Start with ASP selection, because [one compliance guide warns that delay risks bottlenecks and [Price] monthly fines](https://farahsolutions.ae/blog/uae-e-invoicing-guidelines-v1-1-compliance-guide).

Prevention matrix by owner

ViolationOwnerControlEvidence to keep
No system or ASPCFO (Finance)ASP contract signed before your ASP deadlineSigned contract
Late electronic invoiceIT/ERPAutomated transmission with a daily exception reportException reports
Late electronic credit noteFinance (AR)Credit-note workflow routed through the ASPApproval trail
No FTA failure noticeFinanceIncident procedure with a named notifierIncident log
ASP data not updatedFinanceQuarterly master-data reviewChange confirmations
Recipient fails to notify issuerASP and Finance (AP)Contractual alerting SLAASP alerts

A 90-day penalty-avoidance plan

  1. Days 1–30: confirm your phase, scope your entities, shortlist ASPs and run an ERP gap check for PINT AE fields.
  2. Days 31–60: appoint the ASP, map invoice data and configure credit-note flows.
  3. Days 61–90: test end to end during the voluntary window, when no penalties apply. Rehearse the system-failure procedure and train your AP and AR teams.

Dynamics 365 Finance & Operations and Business Central both need ERP-side changes. See upgrading your ERP for Peppol PINT AE compliance.

Frequently Asked Questions

Is e-invoicing mandatory in the UAE?

Yes, for VAT-registered businesses in scope, on a phased timetable under Ministerial Decision No. 244 of 2025. Voluntary adoption opens from 1 July 2026. Businesses with revenue of [Price] million or more must appoint an ASP by 31 July 2026 and go live from 1 January 2027. Our complete guide to e-invoicing in the UAE covers scope.

What is the penalty for not issuing an e-invoice?

Failing to issue and transmit an electronic invoice on time carries a penalty of [Price] per invoice, capped at [Price] per month according to published commentary. If you have not implemented the system or appointed an ASP at all, a separate [Price] monthly penalty can apply as well.

Who is exempted from e-invoicing?

Ministerial Decision No. 243 of 2025 lists the excluded transactions and entities. Being VAT-registered does not by itself put every transaction in scope. Check the official exclusion list against each of your revenue streams. Confirm any borderline cases with your tax adviser before your phase begins, because exclusions apply to specific transactions rather than to whole businesses by default.

What is the penalty for late payment of VAT in the UAE?

Late VAT payment penalties fall under Cabinet Decision No. 40 of 2017 as amended, not under the e-invoicing decision. Rates have changed over time, so take the current figures from the Federal Tax Authority's published penalty schedule. Both penalty regimes can apply to the same business in the same period.

Are there penalties during the voluntary phase?

No. Cabinet Decision No. 106 of 2025 states that its provisions do not apply to businesses that issue, transmit or exchange e-invoices voluntarily. This makes the voluntary window the safest time to test your ERP and ASP connection before your mandatory date.

What penalties apply to notification failures?

The decision covers three notification duties. You must tell the FTA about a system failure and tell your ASP about changes to your registered data. Recipients must tell issuers when they cannot receive. We have not confirmed the amounts or time windows, so read the exact clauses in the MoF decision before you budget for them.

Do e-invoicing penalties apply to credit notes?

Yes. Each electronic credit note not issued and transmitted on time costs [Price] capped at [Price] per month (source). Reported wording suggests this cap sits separately from the invoice cap. Late credit notes could therefore add to invoice penalties in the same month.

Where can I download the UAE e-invoicing penalties PDF?

The official text is the Ministry of Finance's Cabinet Decision on e-invoicing violations and penalties, published as a PDF. The violations table on this page summarises it in plain English. Check the decision itself for any amounts we mark as not confirmed.

In [duration] we confirm your phase and deadline, run an ERP gap check for PINT AE and review your ASP readiness. We then estimate your AED exposure using the worked examples above.

12. Editorial Review

Score58/100 — revise

Strengths

  • Strong information gain: a source-conflict table for deadlines, a per-category vs overall cap reading, a stacking example, and an exposure formula with three costed scenarios.
  • Honest handling of unverified facts: unknown amounts are placeholdered and the AED 1,000 daily figure is flagged in the FAQ instead of being stated.
  • Practical, specific tools: an incident checklist, incident log, prevention matrix with owners, 90-day plan, and a three-question deadline rule.
  • Structure is clear: comparison tables are present, the CTA comes last, and the FAQ uses the Q:/A: format.

Issues raised (addressed by the editor)

  • [high] Three of the six core penalties have no amounts, even though the primary MoF PDF is linked. The page's main promise, 'every fine', is not met. The H1 and meta also say 'every fine'. The 'six violations' framing is unverified.
  • [high] The official dates are all placeholders, and the 'Everyone else' column is thin. The user's most urgent question, 'when do I get hit', is left unanswered. Wafeq, Hawksford and ClearTax appear in the table with no links.
  • [high] The numbers are inconsistent with the stated readings. Under the overall cap of AED 5,000 per month, Scenario A over 6 months should be 30,000, which is consistent, but Scenario C's 'before invoice-level fines' mixes in an undefined total. The opening claim 'up to AED 10,000 a month' contradicts the stacking rule, where the maximum is AED 15,000. The title promises a 'Calculator' but there is only a formula and a table.
  • [high] A primary-law claim is sourced to a third-party blog (abdelhamidcpa) with a date slug of 2026/05/29. Other key numbers are sourced to marketing blogs (covoro, genzone, kaidolab, growacross). The anchor text implies authority it does not have, and the sources are weak.
  • [medium] The answer is hedged and not a direct 40-60 word snippet. The Quick answer says 'capped at AED 5,000 per month' with no hedge, while the section says the cap is disputed.
  • [medium] Both are asserted without a source. The liability claim and the five-corner model rely on a vendor blog. The statement 'If you assume the provider will report the outage, the notification fine falls on you' is unsupported.
  • [medium] It says 'four things' but lists three or four vaguely. The opening claim that 'a late start compounds quickly' is loose, since the fines are flat and do not compound.
  • [medium] There are too many unfilled placeholders for a client-ready draft. Reconsideration deadlines and the exclusions in the FAQ are also unfilled, which weakens the appeal and exemption answers.

Why

  • Demand is real and rising: 880 monthly searches, +85% year on year, and a CPC of $6.85 that shows commercial value. The -18% quarterly dip and the 720 in Aug 2026 look like seasonality, not decline. Mandate deadlines should keep interest high into 2027.

  • Content gaps are clear: competitors disagree on dates (30 Oct 2026 vs 31 July 2026), cover penalties weakly, and give no ASP selection criteria, cost guidance or sector detail. Tech& can win with a dated, cited guide that includes a phase table and a readiness scorecard.

What must change first

  • Open with a 40-70 word direct answer, then use sections that match the PAA questions: mandatory?, who is eligible?, how it works, how to do it. Add FAQ schema.
  • Add a dated 'Which phase am I in?' table with the AED 50M threshold, ASP deadline, go-live and penalty dates, each cited to tax.gov.ae or the MoF. Reconcile the conflicting dates and show a last-updated date.

  • Add a named expert author, plus supporting pages and internal links for 'UAE e-invoicing ASP', 'e-invoicing implementation UAE' and 'e-invoicing penalties UAE'.

Risks / Watch-outs

  • The AI Overview cites tax.gov.ae, PwC and Grant Thornton and will absorb many clicks. Real organic traffic may be well below the 880 volume.
  • Many searchers are researching rather than buying. Lead conversion will be low unless the CTAs are strong. Tech& is also a Dynamics 365 partner, not a tax advisory firm, so E-E-A-T on tax topics is weaker.
